## 2. G2: the cell smoke on the Mac (Appendix S1-B, verbatim)

**The run.** Every cell of B, D and S, both starts: 110 cells, each at its lightest mass with s = 0.
- The hold is 3,000 steps (50 σ-time) and the record 5 periods.
- The worker is the registered one with exactly these two changes: `--wall-hold-steps=3000` and `--target-oscillations=5`.
- The binary is the clean Mac build of f72d764.

**Result: PASS, 110 of 110.** Per cell:
- clean=1 and the exact box;
- η, width and height within 1e-12 of the request (largest relative difference 1.7e-16);
- N_L = N_R, and 0 ties at the load;
- the orientation from the run record, and the [EDMD3-M4C] line where it applies;
- f_pred equal to CC's replication to 8 digits;
- the planned steps = max(10000, ceil(5/ν_pred/dt));
- the files: trace, ψ6 and virial, plus a snapshot where T_snap ≠ 0.

**The first check failed cells by faults of the checker itself.** It is kept as `g2_smoke_output_first_check.txt`. The checker ignored the min-steps clamp, and its f_pred tolerance was finer than the binary's `%.8g` print. The checker was fixed and run again on the same runs with `--check-only`.

Both chunks stopped before "chunk j on" with "STOP: [cn-07-07-07] the stage 1 inputs are not the delivered ones". - macOS `wc -l` pads its counts;
- the expected counts were read from block H's echo command instead of its good line (three failures);

Its one finding for the runsheet: the merge prints a trailing space after reduce_B's last folder line, so block H's filter `trajectories reduced$` left one line per cell in the paste. The filter is now `trajectories reduced *$`.

## 7. Part A: what it would need (decision 39 item 5 e; no code changed)

These are the facts of Report S1-0 (f).
1. **The start and the box in the held path.**
   - gen3's lattice start and exact box are refused outside the speed-of-sound experiment (f72d764 `00ALLINONE.c:22390-22391`). Part A runs `--experiment=energy_transfer`.
   - Part A needs them wired into the held path, or an amendment that accepts the legacy seeder there.
   - At η 0.69 the legacy seeder gives its per-compartment hexagonal fallback at N = 100 and its buffer placement at N = 1600.
2. **The divider's position.**
   - It is a fraction of 2 L0 applied to the box width truncated to the 1/48 σ grid (`:5174`, `:3354`), checked against the request to 1e-5 σ (`:18103`). It stops 60 of the 64 registered cells at initialization; only the π/8 cells pass.
   - Part A needs the position set as an exact length, or a box on the grid. An off-grid position is taken when the box is on the grid (AF4).
3. **`reduce_AF.py`'s window.** The worker passes t0 = 200 as a fixed value (`conf_worker.sh:167`). Stage 1's window [T_eq, T_eq + 5000) needs t0 = T_eq.
4. **The event log.**
   - At N = 1600 one (seed, position) log is 16 MB at η 0.05 and about 590 MB at η 0.69.
   - Only a quarter to a third of its rows are divider rows (D0), and `reduce_AF.py` reads the log whole.
   - A divider-only log, or a reduction that streams, would cut Part A's scratch and memory by about three to four times. Part A's data budget depends on it.
5. **Part A's own gates once the above exists:**
   - its pilot (sec. 4.8.4's seeds-per-position rule);
   - a smoke of every Part A cell and a rehearsal, as G2 and G4 were for Part B.

## 8. For the plan author (information; nothing was changed)

1. **η_int, the interior density from the snapshots.**
   - `reduce_s1_koa.py` uses the strip 2 σ from each wall and the divider, and requires it to be at least 4 σ wide. A compartment's interior is L0 − 4.025 σ wide, so η_int is undefined (NaN, "used 0") wherever L0 < 8.03 σ.
   - At N = 100 (L0 = 3.927/η) that is η > 0.489: the B cells at η 0.50, 0.55, 0.60, 0.63, 0.66, 0.68 and 0.69, and all 10 S cells at N = 100. G4's S cells showed it. At N ≥ 400 every cell is wide enough.
   - There the stiffness identity's static side has no ρ_I from the snapshots. A narrower strip, or the whole compartment, would be the plan author's choice.
2. **The time limits.**
   - Each chunk's limit is 3× the model's prediction, rounded up to a class; the preview measured up to 2.58× the model (median 1.25).
   - A chunk that runs out of time appears in block F as "started and did not finish" and needs the plan author.
   - At the median ratio the campaign would use about 3,250 E5 core-hours.
3. **The verdict on incomplete masses.** The group rule flags a (cell, mass) that loses more than 2 of 25 trajectories (more than 1 of 10 in S), and leaves it out of the cell's Z, ψ6 and stationarity (G4 showed it on the mini data).

## 9. Deviations

1. **The campaign build script.** It is `build_s1_koa.sh`, not f72d764's `build_gen3_koa.sh`, which refuses the new clone. The three changes are in section 6, and the build rehearsal ran four times.
2. **`record_s1_build.sh` sources the campaign clone's `koa_env.sh`.** Without it, the record's compiler line would have named the sandbox shell's gcc. Found in CC's review: the Mac rehearsal cannot see it, because its gcc shim prints 14.3.0.
3. **The gate runsheet updates the scripts clone to `0969b0ed`**, later than the rehearsed `61a0a933`. The gate's job scripts are unchanged between the two (section 6).
4. **Stage 1's block B is completed after the gate.** It names AFTER_THE_GATE until the stage 1 commit exists; the generator then writes it with that commit and the three new files' SHA-256.
5. **The arrays: one per (group, time class)** (one array has one time limit).
   - A group's throttles add up to at most 64. They are given greedily by predicted core-hours per slot, which balances the classes' finishing times.
   - The groups run one after another, in the registered order: the next group's block E follows a clean block H.
   - This is CC's reading of "at most 64 running at once over all stage 1 arrays" and of the order. Using 128 needs the plan author's go on a pasted sinfo line.
   - The `--array` lists are long (up to 819 characters, B1600's 3-hour class) because the chunk table keeps the task list's order. Renumbering the chunks per class would have raised the time class of 802 chunks. The lists are written by the generator; G4 ran the generator's lines for the mini table.
6. **The merge, chunk-check and resubmission scripts are Test R's with listed changes:**
   - per group: the chunk table names the group's chunks;
   - the run's virial and snapshot files are moved;
   - `reduce_s1_koa.py` runs after reduce_B;
   - one resubmission line per time class.
7. **G2's first check** (section 2).
8. **The rehearsals' shims**, listed in each output (Appendix S1-D; the gate rehearsal's output in `gate_rehearsal/`).
9. **A rule slip of mine.**
   - To rerun G3 in the same scratch folder, I ran `rm -rf -- "$SP/d39/g3tmp"` twice. That deleted my own scratch copies without asking.
   - I also made an empty folder in `hspist3/` and removed it.
   - No one else's data and no tracked file were touched.
10. **Scratch folders left in place, nothing deleted:**
    - the selftest folders `vself` to `vself4`, 12 MB each;
    - G4 run 1, 43 MB;
    - the two build rehearsals, 28 and 29 MB;
    - the gate rehearsal, 88 MB;
    - G2, 17 MB.
11. **The cost columns.** The design prints E5 core-hours, with ×1.5 as the registration's margin. The registration's planning column is E5 core-hours too. An earlier labelling in the work in progress was corrected before this report.
12. **The verdict's new orientation column** (section 5).
13. **Chris's 5000-disk window run** (decision 39's note) changed the tracked `hspist3/wall_position.csv` and `energy_log.csv` in the engine-gen3 worktree. They are not committed: a window run is a demonstration, never data (decision 39 item 4).

## 8. Choices left to CC by the registration, as made here

- streams numbered in the task list's order; the mass index = the alpha's index in the nine-mass ladder
- the stride's KR frequency with N_s = N/2 of the cell (Test G's x_of assumed N_s = 50)
- the time limit rounded up to a class (one array has one time limit); the arrays formed per group and class
- the task line's 15 fields and the folder layout stage1/gen3/<cell id>/m_<M> (Test R's merge layout, the engine level gen3)
- the record steps and T_rec of a (cell, mass) replicate the binary's own --target-oscillations arithmetic; Delta_psi and T_snap use that T_rec

##CHRIS 2026-10-12 (plan-author decision 39 item 5 f; registration 261012 sec. 4.8.8 and 4.8.10) -- THE STAGE 1 RUNSHEET (KOA), Part B
(blocks B, D and S). Written by cluster/gen3_stage1_261012/make_s1_runsheet.py from the chunk table and the task list. PREPARED: typed
only after the gate (G6 (c), (d)), its verdict, the stage 1 commit and the plan author's go (G7). Chris types every command.

WHAT IT DOES. Each chunk is one array task of one core: it runs its task lines (consecutive lines of one (cell, mass)) one after the
other with s1_worker_koa.sh and the campaign binary ~/harddisks_gen3_s1/hspist3/00ALLINONE (00ALLINONE  git f72d764  target koa), checked at start
against the committed build record and the build-time record, without git. Chunk 1 runs alone first (G6 (e)); then the groups in
the registered order (sec. 4.8.8): B100, B400, D, B900, S, B1600. A group's arrays are one per time class (--time is the class), typed together;
their throttles add up to 64 at most, so at most 64 chunks run at once over all stage 1 arrays (sec. 4.8.8; 128 only with the plan
author's go on a pasted sinfo line with >= 10 % of the nodes idle). The next group's arrays are typed only after the previous group's
merge has ended. The merge (one per group) checks every chunk's log and folder, plans before it moves anything, reduces every
(cell, mass) and compresses the traces; the fetch brings the summaries, run records, logs, every snapshot and the whole (N 100, pi/8) cell.
KOA RULES, as before: the login node only for light commands; squeue before every sbatch; never submit a job twice; nothing in
~/harddisks, ~/harddisks_resched, ~/harddisks_resched2, ~/harddisks_gen3 or ~/harddisks_gen3_s1 changes; every job stops with
"STOP: [<node>] ..." on a failed start-up check before it runs anything: paste the STOP line, change nothing, wait. NO GIT AT RUN
TIME; every sbatch file carries --exclude=cn-05-08-07. THE SANDBOX LINE is the established one: srun -p sandbox -t 1:00:00 -c 2 --mem=4G --pty /bin/bash
THE STAGE 1 COMMIT does not exist yet: after the gate's fetch CC copies the fetched build-time record s1_gate/BUILD_RECORD_S1.txt to
cluster/gen3_stage1_261012/build_record_f72d764.txt, writes the two manifests (make_s1_manifests.py), commits, and writes this runsheet
again with that commit (--commit): block B then names it and holds the SHA-256 lines of those three files. Nothing else changes.

    "chunk logs: 1462; chunks done: 1462 of 1462 (group B1600; each with its folder and exactly one log with 'chunk <j> done'); earlier attempts: 0 stopped in the start-up checks, 0 started and not finished"
    "group B1600 without a done log: stopped once 0, stopped twice or more 0, started and not finished 0, no log 0" and "nothing to resubmit"

S1 BLOCK H-B1600 (KOA login node, when squeue -u charing lists no gen3-s1-merge-B1600 job; paste everything):
squeue -u charing
L=$(ls -t ~/harddisks_gen3_k2/hspist3/logs/gen3-s1-merge-B1600_*.out | head -1) && echo "$L"
grep -v -e '^| stage1/gen3/' -e '^reduce_B stage1/gen3/.*: exit 0 ' -e 'trajectories reduced *$' -e '^reduce_s1 stage1/gen3/.*: exit 0 ' -e ': [0-9]* traces compressed$' "$L"
echo "destinations $(grep -c '^| stage1/gen3/' "$L"); reduce_B exit 0: $(grep -c '^reduce_B stage1/gen3/.*: exit 0 ' "$L"); reduce_s1 exit 0: $(grep -c '^reduce_s1 stage1/gen3/.*: exit 0 ' "$L"); compressed: $(grep -c ': [0-9]* traces compressed$' "$L")"
grep -h '^reduce_s1 ' "$L" | grep -o '([0-9]* fit failures)' | sort | uniq -c
cat /mnt/lustre/koa/scratch/charing/harddisks_gen3/stage1/B1600/merged/.merge_complete
ls /mnt/lustre/koa/scratch/charing/harddisks_gen3/stage1/B1600/job_logs | wc -l
